# Automated suite usefulness audit — 2026-07

- Audit date: 2026-07-25
- Process-safety re-audit: 2026-07-26
- Audited base: `ceb573204be9ecd94172a21686e0bcf5d8a42eca` (the then-current
  `origin/main`, including the post-`f12c19a` Lightning damage bridge)
- Re-audit migration base:
  `6081dcab45b987f88f94c12d3861c87e73ab73ab`
- Home platform: Windows game processes driven from WSL Linux
- Remote platform: one isolated GE-Proton 10-34 game peer on the NFO box

This audit inventories every discovered automated unit, static, launcher,
native, workspace/build, and `tools/` entry point. A check earns `USEFUL` only
when it pins named behavior and has a reachable rejection path. `WEAK` means
the check is real but materially self-referential or indirect. `VACUOUS`
means it cannot reject or asserts no behavior. `STALE` means the behavior or
execution model is no longer admissible.

No test was deleted. The 58 legacy live entry points retained every existing
behavior check and were migrated onto the shared exact-PID plus exact staged
executable ownership ledger. Import, `--help`, and argument-failure contracts
now pin zero process operations across all 58. No removal was authorized; the
separate dispositions table found no complete modern-harness duplicate.

The recovered preliminary handoff count was 851. The original audit completed
at 859 rows. Current main added one Static RE contract, and this process-safety
re-audit adds 19 executable Python contracts, bringing the current inventory
to 879 checks. The original detailed tables remain below, with the 58 tool
verdicts updated in place; the re-audit contract delta is listed separately.

## Repairs made during the audit

1. **Lobby-state label contract.** The static lifecycle check merely searched
   for three return strings, so a branch-remapping mutant survived. It now
   requires the `NotInGame`, `InHub`, and `InBoneyard` cases and their labels
   in exact order. The original survivor and the post-fix rejection are
   archived under `mutations/static-re-mutations-initial.log` and
   `mutations/static-lobby-branch-fix-mutation.log`.
2. **Native Lua content registry in CI.** The native registry binary existed
   but was absent from the authoring workflow. The workflow now compiles and
   runs production `lua_content_registry.cpp`, and the static identity
   contract pins that CI step.
3. **Workspace process ownership.** `Verify-Workspace.ps1` previously stopped
   a PID based only on the process name. Cleanup now resolves
   `Win32_Process.ExecutablePath` and refuses anything outside the exact staged
   executable. The Python process-isolation contract pins the path check.
4. **Seeded layout visual gate.** The prior gate demanded byte-identical
   samples across animated backbuffers and selected decor targets before
   proving that a 310–330-unit traversable parking point existed. Healthy
   captures failed while still reporting zero temporal-envelope divergence.
   The repaired gate preselects targets against the real nav grid, tolerates
   at most the native collision radius after placement, retains an exact
   zero-gap temporal-envelope comparison under the real complex-lighting
   profile, and adds a stable-geometry profile with:
   1,024 visible-pixel and 128-color quorums; at most 2/255 within-peer
   temporal movement and cross-peer temporal-envelope gap; at least 2,000
   edges per peer; and at least 0.985 symmetric edge agreement within two
   pixels. Persistent edge geometry is derived from the temporal minimum so
   peer-local bright sway peaks cannot accumulate into different shapes. The
   raw stable fraction is diagnostic only because swaying native foliage
   repeatedly moved it below 50% while the exact envelope and geometry agreed.
   A 3/255 mutation,
   a large missing-decor mutation, and an all-dark input all reject. The
   renewed real gate retained exact zero-gap temporal envelopes, bounded
   stable pixels, high-agreement edge geometry, and exact-PID/path cleanup.
5. **Post-placement native-nav validation.** A native-nav sample reported as
   traversable can still be collision-relocated after actor rebind. One real
   scenery-prop capture moved the owner 515 world units into the protected
   decor ROI. The verifier now tries the existing ranked 310–330-unit samples
   in order and accepts only a sample whose actual settled owner transforms
   preserve the unchanged radial and ROI bounds. If every sample for the
   densest decor target is collision-relocated outside those bounds, the next
   spatially distinct target in that family is validated. Every rejected
   placement and target is retained in the run evidence. Disabling either
   retry makes its focused regression reject.
6. **Layout capture survival and transient-frame handling.** A 10-run gate
   reached a real ground-clutter capture with the host surrounded by stock
   enemies and nearly dead; the raw D3D9 frame was dominated by the native red
   damage flash and correctly failed the unchanged 1,000-color quality gate.
   Layout-only launches now use the existing owner HP/MP survival guard, which
   does not alter seeded decor or render inputs, and each required frame gets
   bounded retries on only the explicit low-information verdict. Retry frames
   use distinct evidence paths; transport and other capture failures still
   propagate immediately. Mutants disabling the retry or survival guard both
   reject.
7. **Aggregate stable-profile capture retry.** A later 10-run gate reached a
   real tree area whose first 16-frame simple-lighting batch retained only 642
   stable visible pixels against the unchanged 1,024-pixel quorum, although
   its cross-peer temporal envelope was exact and its persistent edge score
   was 0.9953. The verifier now retries only an aggregate batch that fails the
   existing stable-content quorum, with three bounded attempts and distinct
   evidence paths. A subsequent real large-rock target stayed below the quorum
   across all three batches (747, 809, and 856 visible pixels); after exhausting
   the aggregate retry, that target is now rejected and the next ranked,
   spatially distinct, placement-safe target is captured. The verifier still
   fails immediately on any content-bearing pixel, envelope, edge, camera, or
   transport mismatch; no threshold was reduced. Replaying the rejected tree
   batch followed by a content-bearing batch accepts attempt two, while mutants
   that accept the first insufficient batch or stop before the next decor
   target are rejected by focused regressions.
   In the final repetition, run-seven ground clutter rejected two
   content-insufficient batches and accepted the third only after it met the
   unchanged stable-content quorum.
8. **Pre-placement actor-parking snapshot.** A later repetition correctly
   rejected its first parking sample after native collision displaced both
   actors into the protected decor ROI. The old retry then re-enumerated the
   nav grid after moving those actors and treated an occupancy-driven candidate
   count change from two to three as verifier corruption. Parking retries now
   iterate one ranked pre-placement nav snapshot. Every candidate still has to
   satisfy the unchanged 310–330-unit pre-placement band, post-placement native
   collision tolerance, and protected-ROI clearance. A mutant that re-queries
   the candidate set after the first rejected placement is rejected by the
   focused regression.
9. **Low-edge-content batch retry.** A later real tree capture had 1,958 host
   and 1,955 client persistent edges, just below the unchanged 2,000-edge
   content quorum, while the symmetric match fraction was 0.99796 against the
   unchanged 0.985 minimum. The same bounded batch and alternate-target path
   now treats either stable-pixel or edge-content insufficiency as a request
   for more content, while content-bearing mismatches still reject. A replay
   rejected that batch, then accepted a 3,631/3,635-edge batch on attempt two;
   a mutant that accepts the first low-edge batch is rejected.
10. **Ambient-wave isolation for the static-layout gate.** Run six of a later
   repetition reached synchronized ground clutter with exact zero-gap temporal
   envelopes and sufficient stable content, but the persistent-edge score was
   0.98427 against the unchanged 0.985 minimum. The mismatch image localized
   the difference to a stock skeleton crossing the decor ROI in peer-local
   animation poses, not to the seeded decor. The verifier now activates the
   existing semantic manual-enemy-spawner test mode on both peers after hub
   convergence and before run entry, and requires both peers to acknowledge
   that it is active. This removes unrelated combat actors at source without
   changing a visual threshold or masking pixels. A mutant that disables the
   mode is rejected by the static ordering and activation contract. At the
   same run-six/ground-clutter boundary in the final repetition, 7,611 host
   and 7,361 client persistent edges passed at 0.99613 symmetric agreement
   against the unchanged 0.985 minimum.
11. **Complex-lighting target content.** Run nine of a later repetition
   selected a scenery prop under a dense stock tree. Its peer envelopes were
   bit-exact (zero differing pixels and zero channel gap), but only 122 host
   and 124 client visible ROI pixels remained against the unchanged 512-pixel
   complex-profile quorum. A target that is dark but exact now advances to the
   next ranked, placement-safe prop just like the simple-profile content path.
   Any nonzero envelope difference remains a hard failure and cannot enter
   that retry. The focused regression drives a complex-content retry followed
   by a simple-content retry before accepting a third candidate; a mutant that
   disconnects complex content from target retry is rejected.
12. **Legacy verifier owned-process migration.** All 58 owner-listed entry
   points now acquire process ownership only from launcher-returned exact PIDs
   paired with exact `stage\SolomonDark.exe` paths. The shared ledger validates
   every identity through an exact `Win32_Process` PID query before adoption
   and revalidates the full set before any stop. A changed path refuses the
   entire cleanup set. Empty ledgers, imports, `--help`, and argument failures
   perform no process query or stop. Existing verifier assertions and
   rejection paths were not changed.

### Launcher contract host note

The launcher contract project targets `net8.0-windows` and x86. Its
authoritative final run therefore used the Windows .NET host. An initial
Linux-x64 host invocation rejected the x86 assembly before executing any
contract; that invalid-host diagnostic is preserved separately and is not
counted as a suite result:
`/mnt/d/codex-evidence/suite-audit-20260725/home-post-rebase-final/launcher-contracts-linux-host-invalid.log`.

## NFO platform scope and cleanup

The user explicitly waived duplicate Python/static execution on the NFO box:
the same pure-Python code already ran under WSL Linux, and the static RE
registry requires the real game binary, which was not copied merely to rerun
source checks. They are recorded as **redundant with WSL Linux execution**,
not as passes. Windows launcher/build/workspace gates are platform
inapplicable on the NFO host.

The independent NFO signal was a true two-machine game session. One private
GE-Proton 10-34 runtime, prefix, game copy, loader copy, and Xvfb display ran
under `/root/codex-suite-audit-20260725-TIPUuW0t`; no package, service,
tenant Steam tree, tenant prefix, or tenant display was touched. The NFO peer
injected the loader and exchanged live game transport with the Home peer.
The 50-packet path sample had 0% loss, 26–28 ms RTT, 26.56 ms mean RTT,
0.535 ms population deviation, and 0.592 ms mean absolute successive jitter.

The session helper's own final `ok=false` is a cleanup-probe false negative:
it saw the exact SSH stop-marker shell that was waiting for it. The independent
post-stop probe reports no staged process and no external stage reference.
The post-removal probe reports zero game binaries, zero Proton runtime files,
and zero stage processes; only `capability-probe.log` and `cleanup.log`
remain remotely.

create-selection, runtime profile, Lua bot, and cast tables. The inventory,
hashes, IDs, modes, and hold durations are in `element-inventory.json`.

on both peers when the archived chosen frames were manually inspected.

### Issue #52 resolution — 2026-07-27

The historical table above is retained as the audit observation. The
client-Earth magnitude was later isolated as **CLIENT-ONLY INFLATION**, not
stock balance: beta.18 wrote the unrelated `1956` primary-stat normalization
into the packet-driven replay Boulder's native damage fields, and the host
allowed that observer replay to apply a second contact beside the client's
stock claim. The exact six-cell post-fix matrix makes the 2-frame, 170-frame,
and full-charge host/client endpoints bit-identical while preserving the
pre-fix host stock endpoints. Full trace and evidence:
[`earth-boulder-damage-formula-2026-07-27.md`](../reverse-engineering/earth-boulder-damage-formula-2026-07-27.md).

## Live spawn/death/spectate lifecycle

### Home-PC loopback pair

Overall verdict: **FAIL with product findings**.

- Spawn/materialization and the host-victim organic path passed. A stock enemy
  dealt a real 2.961914 raw-HP decrement; owner HP reached zero at 0.264 s,
  the native death presentation began at 1.427 s, and spectating/red-clear
  completed at 6.423 s.
- Both archived host-death frames visibly show the stock textured corpse/staff
  presentation. Corpse position stayed exact on both peers, dead input caused
  no cast/mana/world-effect activity, and host spectator controls stayed on
  the surviving client.
- The client-victim path failed twice before death: the selected stock
  native-wave enemy dispatched its attack but produced no client raw-HP
  decrement. Client death animation, client-to-host corpse presentation, the
  reverse spectate path, and reverse-direction enemy reacquisition therefore
  could not be accepted.
- The independent Game Over/lobby continuation verifier passed its
  three-participant lifecycle: Game Over, Loading Boneyard, same-lobby return,
  and a second run with the same processes. Its terminal setup is queued/
  synthetic, so it is evidence for post-match continuity, not a substitute
  for the failed organic client death.

Primary evidence:

- `live-loopback/lifecycle/host-terminal/result.json`
- `live-loopback/lifecycle/host-terminal/death-animation/`
- `live-loopback/lifecycle/client/result.json`
- `live-loopback/lifecycle/client/result-r2.json`
- `live-loopback/lifecycle/game-over/result.json`

### two-machine real-WAN (Home PC + NFO/GE-Proton)

Overall verdict: **FAIL with product findings**.

- Both peers took organic stock-enemy damage (Home host 5.993164 raw HP; NFO
  client 5.846680 raw HP) and progressed through death presentation and
  spectating. Host timings were 1.823 s presentation and 9.702 s spectator;
  client timings were 0.932 s and 9.407 s.
- Both strict lifecycle results rejected the same mismatch: the owner retained
  a tiny positive sentinel HP (`0.001`–`0.003`) while the observer reported
  exact zero, so the required owner `hp_zero_seconds` milestone never existed.
- Manual visual review found each local victim's stock textured corpse, but
  remote presentation failed: the NFO observer rendered the dead Home host as
  a black silhouette, and the Home observer did not render the dead NFO client
  corpse.
- Independent post-spectator probes passed in both directions: dead-input
  lockout, spectator control stability, semantic corpse persistence at the
  same actor/position, and no illicit cast/mana/world-effect activity.
- Enemy reacquisition passed: all 13 samples targeted the surviving Home host,
  never the dead NFO participant.
- The authoritative Game Over command/nonce converged on both peers. Home
  rendered the stock Game Over title; NFO reported the native Boneyard-fade
  presentation. The lobby returned and a fresh second run (`1046712125` after
  `168833028`) started with the same Home/NFO process IDs, no rejoin/relaunch,
  two ready participants, and both actors materialized.
- **Loading Boneyard visual finding:** neither peer displayed the text barrier.
  High-frame-rate recordings found the real fade-to-black interval on each
  peer, but no matching title pixels; manually inspected before/black/after
  frames confirm the absence.

Primary evidence:

- `live-wan/lifecycle/host/result-final.json`
- `live-wan/lifecycle/client/result-final.json`
- `live-wan/lifecycle/host/post-spectator-probes.json`
- `live-wan/lifecycle/client/post-spectator-probes.json`
- `live-wan/lifecycle/client/enemy-reacquire-survivor.json`
- `live-wan/lifecycle/post-match/result.json`
- `live-wan/lifecycle/post-match/continuation-second-run.json`
- `live-wan/lifecycle/post-match/fourth-run-video-scan.json`
- `live-wan/lifecycle/post-match/fifth-run-video-scan.json`
- `visual-review/lifecycle-contact-sheet.png`
- `visual-review/loading-boneyard-contact-sheet.png`

## 2026-07-26 legacy process-safety evidence

The side-effect-free Python audit-hook probe executed three benign paths for
each migrated entry point: import dry-run, `--help`, and an invalid argument.
All 174 invocations returned the required status and recorded zero
`subprocess.Popen`, `os.system`, spawn, or kill events. The probe never
launched a game, queried a game process, or contacted a Lua pipe.

Evidence:

- `/mnt/d/codex-evidence/legacy-guard-20260726/entry-point-probes/summary.json`
- `/mnt/d/codex-evidence/legacy-guard-20260726/entry-point-probes/records/`

## Legacy live entry points — exact-ownership re-audit

All 58 entry points below now use the shared complete ownership ledger. Process
identities are acquired as launcher-returned exact PIDs plus exact staged
executable paths, validated through `Win32_Process.ExecutablePath`, and
revalidated before a two-phase exact-ID stop. No entry point enumerates or
stops a process by bare name. The executable import, `--help`, and
argument-failure probe covers all 174 benign paths with zero process-operation
events. No verifier was deleted and no behavior assertion was changed.

The detailed duplication comparison is in
`legacy-verifier-dispositions-2026-07.md`. No complete duplicate was found, so
all 58 remain retained pending any separately approved owner decision.
